<div class="bh-vi">
<section class="title wrap">
  <h1>Visual identity</h1>
  <p class="lede">Claret and gold, a portcullis from Westminster, and lettering from our own club gazette. Almost everything about how Belgrave looks today was already there in the archive. This page tells that story, then sets out the colours, kit and crests for anyone making something for the club.</p>
  <div class="jump"><a href="#colours">Colours</a><a href="#crests">Crests</a><a href="#type">Typography</a><a href="#using">Using the identity</a></div>
</section>

<!-- COLOURS -->
<section class="chapter" id="colours">
  <div class="wrap">
    <header>
      <span class="label">Colours</span>
      <h2>Claret and gold</h2>
      <p>The claret can’t be red or purple. The gold can’t be yellow or orange. It took the club seventeen years to arrive at them, and Belgrave has raced in claret and gold ever since, though the shades have varied.</p>
    </header>

    <div class="kits">
      <div class="kit"><div class="sw"><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/era-1887.jpg" alt="Belgrave Harriers club card for the 1902–3 season, listing the club colours as dark and light blue"></div><div><b>1887 – 1903</b><span>Club colours were dark and light blue, as the club’s printed card for the 1902–3 season records. No kit is known to survive.</span></div></div>
      <div class="kit"><div class="sw"><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/era-1904.jpg" alt="Four Belgrave runners in hooped jerseys and long shorts"></div><div><b>1904 – 1920</b><span>After one season in “all white, blue trimmings”, claret and gold arrive as hoops on the jersey.</span></div></div>
      <div class="kit"><div class="sw"><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/era-1920.jpg" alt="A Belgrave club group of athletes and officials"></div><div><b>1920 – 1932</b><span>Claret jersey, gold hoop, the circular crest often sewn on the hoop, long white shorts.</span></div></div>
      <div class="kit"><div class="sw"><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/era-1932.jpg" alt="Belgrave runners wearing relay numbers A2 and A3 in 1965, in singlets with a single hoop"></div><div><b>1932 – 1982</b><span>Claret singlet with a gold hoop. This team photo is from 1965.</span></div></div>
      <div class="kit"><div class="sw"><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/era-today.jpg" alt="Belgrave women in today's claret and gold singlets at a cross country race"></div><div><b>Today</b><span>Claret unchanged from the previous kit; the gold moved from the yellow and amber of recent decades to a more metallic tone.</span></div></div>
    </div>

    <div class="split">
      <figure><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/westminster-arms.jpg" alt="The coat of arms of the City of Westminster: a blue shield with a gold portcullis and chains" style="max-width:380px"><figcaption>The arms of the City of Westminster. The founders took the portcullis and its chains for the Belgrave badge. From Alan Mead’s archive.</figcaption></figure>
      <div class="prose measure">
        <h3>A clue in the portcullis</h3>
        <p>For a club now known for claret and gold, the first sixteen years are a puzzle. The founding colours were written down as dark and light blue, and nothing survives to prove it either way.</p>
        <p>In support of the blue, though, is the coat of arms of the City of Westminster, from which our crest’s portcullis was taken. Westminster is home to Belgravia and to the club’s founding on the north bank of the Thames.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="card-band">
    <div class="wrap">
      <figure><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/c1933.jpg" alt="The Belgravian, April–May 1933: a claret card cover with the title and crest printed in gold"><figcaption><span class="label">The Belgravian · April–May 1933</span>Claret card, gold ink.</figcaption></figure>
      <div>
        <span class="label">From the gazette</span>
        <h3>Gold on claret, ninety years ago</h3>
        <p>The Belgravian, the club’s own gazette, was printed on claret card from 1928 to 1939. In the early 1930s its title and crest were printed in gold ink. It is the same pairing we use for the website header, honours and anything ceremonial today.</p>
        <p>Measured from a faded scan, the card is in the same claret family as our kit and screen colours.</p>
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="wrap">

    <div class="swatches" aria-label="Our four core colours">
      <div class="swatch"><i style="background:#7a001d"></i><div><b>Claret</b>Kit, header, links</div></div>
      <div class="swatch"><i style="background:#d6b97a"></i><div><b>Gold</b>On claret only: crest, lettering, honours</div></div>
      <div class="swatch"><i style="background:#7a5e1c"></i><div><b>Gold ink</b>Labels and datelines on light backgrounds</div></div>
      <div class="swatch"><i style="background:#f5efe4"></i><div><b>Cream</b>Callouts and heritage panels</div></div>
    </div>
  </div>
</section>

<!-- CRESTS -->
<section class="chapter" id="crests">
  <div class="wrap">
    <header>
      <span class="label">Crests</span>
      <h2>Cross keys and portcullis</h2>
      <p>Club rules state that the badge shall be Cross Keys and Portcullis with the motto Semper Paratus: always ready. Since 1887 it has been drawn inside a circle, a wreath and a shield.</p>
    </header>

    <div class="split">
      <figure><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/roundel-1957.jpg" alt="The circular crest printed in The Belgravian, 1957, with FOUNDED 1887 underneath" style="max-width:420px"><figcaption><span class="label">The Belgravian · 1957</span>The circular crest as the gazette printed it, with FOUNDED 1887 set in spaced capitals underneath.</figcaption></figure>
      <div class="prose measure">
        <h3>The circle</h3>
        <p>A ring lettered BELGRAVE HARRIERS around crossed keys, the letters B and H, the year split 18 and 87, and a chained portcullis. It appears on the gazette from 1928, and versions of it were sewn onto club jerseys for much of the twentieth century.</p>
        <p>Today’s circular crest is that same badge, redrawn so it holds up small on a singlet or a phone screen. It is the club’s main mark.</p>
        <div class="founded"><svg aria-hidden="true" focusable="false" class="founded-mark" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" version="1.1" viewBox="287 287 926 926">
  <g fill="currentColor">

        <p>The FOUNDED 1887 line under the crest comes straight from the gazette pages of the 1930s and 1960s.</p>

    <div class="timeline">
      <h3>The roundel through the years</h3>
      <p>The badge was settled at the club’s inaugural meeting in 1887: a portcullis from the arms of the City of Westminster, with the motto Semper Paratus. By the early 1900s it had moved inside a ring lettered BELGRAVE HARRIERS, and that roundel has been redrawn by each generation since.</p>
      <div class="rounds">
        <figure><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/roundel-1887.jpg" alt="Early shield badge with portcullis, crossed keys and the motto Semper Paratus"><figcaption><b>1880s</b>The first badge, as drawn on Alan Mead’s archive site. He notes the crossed keys were a later addition.</figcaption></figure>
        <figure><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/roundel-1902.jpg" alt="Roundel printed on a blue club publication cover, 1902–03"><figcaption><b>1902–03</b>The earliest roundel we have found, on a club publication cover.</figcaption></figure>
        <figure><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/roundel-1933.jpg" alt="Roundel in gold ink on the claret card cover of The Belgravian, 1933"><figcaption><b>1933</b>Gold ink on claret card. The Belgravian, April–May.</figcaption></figure>
        <figure><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/roundel-1936.jpg" alt="Roundel with FOUNDED 1887 on The Belgravian, August 1936"><figcaption><b>1936</b>With FOUNDED 1887 beneath. The Belgravian, August.</figcaption></figure>
        <figure><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/roundel-1957sq.jpg" alt="Roundel with FOUNDED 1887 printed in The Belgravian, 1957"><figcaption><b>1957</b>The Belgravian, on austerity paper.</figcaption></figure>
        <figure><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/roundel-1970.jpg" alt="Roundel in red on the yellow cover of The Belgravian, June 1970"><figcaption><b>1970</b>Red on yellow. The Belgravian, June.</figcaption></figure>

<!-- TYPOGRAPHY -->
<section class="chapter" id="type">
  <div class="wrap">
    <header>
      <span class="label">Typography</span>
      <h2>Lettering from The Belgravian</h2>
      <p>Our headlines are set in Castoro Belgrave, a typeface adapted from the open-source Castoro and redrawn towards the 1960s mastheads of the club gazette.</p>
    </header>

    <div class="pair">
      <figure><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/scan-1960.jpg" alt="The Belgravian masthead, May 1960, in light classical capitals"><figcaption><span class="label">The Belgravian · May 1960</span>Light, widely spaced classical capitals.</figcaption></figure>
      <figure><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/scan-1966.jpg" alt="The Belgravian masthead, August 1966, in heavier capitals on yellow"><figcaption><span class="label">The Belgravian · August 1966</span>The same kind of capital, heavier.</figcaption></figure>
    </div>

    <div class="split">
      <div class="mast" role="img" aria-label="THE BELGRAVIAN lettering lifted from the May 1960 cover, in gold on claret"><i></i></div>
      <div class="prose measure">
        <h3>The look of Belgravia</h3>
        <p>By May 1960 the gazette’s title was set in inscriptional capitals, the kind you see engraved on brass plates and honours boards. It is also a look that belongs to Belgravia, the London district whose name the club shares.</p>
      </div>
    </div>

    <div class="split rev">
      <div class="specimen" aria-label="Castoro Belgrave specimen">
        <span class="big">Belgravian</span>
        <span class="hl">Men and women sweep Surrey League titles</span>
      </div>
      <div class="prose measure">
        <h3>Castoro Belgrave</h3>
        <p>Castoro is an open-source serif, close in spirit to the 1960 masthead, redrawn towards the gazette.</p>
        <p>There are two Titling cuts of tall capitals for page titles and big moments: Titling Light, matched to the May 1960 cover, and Titling, matched to August 1966. The heavier weights set headlines, where they need to stand out from the text beneath.</p>
      </div>
    </div>

    <div class="tweaks">
      <div><span class="glyph">A</span><p>A low crossbar, about a quarter of the way up, measured from the 1960 cover.</p></div>
      <div><span class="glyph">E</span><p>A middle bar that ends square, with no serif.</p></div>
      <div><span class="glyph">G</span><p>A G that sits inside one oval, with only half a spur, redrawn after the 1960 G.</p></div>
    </div>

    <dl class="stack" aria-label="How the typefaces are used">
      <div><dt class="s1">Belgrave</dt><p>Castoro Belgrave Titling. Page titles, homepage features, the club name.</p></div>
      <div><dt class="s2">Headlines</dt><p>Castoro Belgrave. Articles, news cards, the history page.</p></div>
      <div><dt class="s3">Navigation and labels</dt><p>Franklin Gothic. Menus, buttons, section labels and subheadings.</p></div>
      <div><dt class="s4">Everything you read</dt><p>Public Sans. Body text and captions.</p></div>
      <div><dt class="s5">2.BELGRAVE H 2:25:43</dt><p>IBM Plex Mono. Results, times and records, set in fixed-width columns as The Belgravian printed them when it was typewritten in the 1960s and 70s.</p></div>
    </dl>

    <figure style="margin-top:20px"><img src="https://stephengardner1.github.io/belgrave-harriers-data/images/visual-identity/results-1966.jpg" alt="Typewritten results from The Belgravian, August 1966: the Southampton to Bournemouth relay, Portsmouth first and Belgrave Harriers second in 2:25:43, and the Warwickshire County Championships 3 miles won by Geoff North" style="border:1px solid var(--rule)"><figcaption><span class="label">The Belgravian · August 1966</span>Typed results from the Southampton–Bournemouth relay and the Warwickshire County Championships.</figcaption></figure>
  </div>
</section>
